) to each piece. We get that upon swapping, the difference to the right hand side is at most
O(n^{1/2-\delta})
, so that

(5.14)

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})&\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\operatorname{GinOE},r}^{(1,0)}(x)F_{-}(x/\sqrt{n})dx-O(n^{1/2-\delta})\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+10\epsilon,1-10\epsilon]}(G_{n})-O(n^{1/2-\delta^{\prime}})\end{aligned},

where
G_{n}
is the real Ginibre matrix. In
[
14
]
, Corollary 4.3 Edelman, Kostlan and Shub obtained the marginal probability density
f_{n}(x),x\in\mathbb{R}
of the real eigenvalues of
G_{n}
, and in Corollary 4.5 they proved that
f_{n}(x)
converges to
\frac{1}{2}
for
|x|<1,
to
(2+\sqrt{2})/8
for
|x|=1
and to 0 for
|x|>1
. The convergence is both pointwise and in
L^{p}
norms for
1\leq p<\infty
. That is, asymptotically the density of real eigenvalues of
G_{n}
is uniform on
[-1,1]
. By computations in
[
14
]
, Chapter 5 they obtained
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{R}}(G_{n})=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n}}+O(1)
. Combining all these facts, we get that
\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+10\epsilon,1-10\epsilon]}(G_{n})=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{n}}(1-10\epsilon)(1+o(1))
. Taking this back into (
5.14
), we get

(5.15)

\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})\geq\sqrt{\frac{2n}{\pi}}(1-10\epsilon)(1+o(1))-O(n^{1/2-\delta^{\prime}}).

Next we get an upper bound for the variance of
N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})
. The situation is more complicated here as (
5.13
) implies universality only in the bulk, so we cannot directly use the result on the variance of
N_{\mathbb{R}}(G_{n})
. We denote by

I_{1}:=[-1+\epsilon+n^{-1/4},1-\epsilon-n^{-1/4}],I_{2}:=[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon],I_{3}:=[-1+\epsilon-n^{-1/4},1-\epsilon+n^{-1/4}].

This time, we consider smooth, nonnegative functions
R_{-}(x),R_{+}(x)
satisfying

\displaystyle 1_{I_{1}}(x)\leq R_{-}(x/\sqrt{n})\leq 1_{I_{2}}(x)\leq R_{+}(x/\sqrt{n})\leq 1_{I_{3}}(x).

Moreover, we shall choose
R_{+}(x)
and
R_{-}(x)
in such a way that they can be chopped into the sum of
O(\sqrt{n})
smooth functions with support length
O(1)
each, so the previous computations still work here.

We begin with some observations. First, the previous proof already implies, using
|a^{2}-b^{2}|=|a-b||a+b|
,

(5.16)

|(\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n}))^{2}-(\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(G_{n}))^{2}|=O(n^{1-\delta^{\prime}}).

Using the fact that
f_{n}(x)
is approximately uniform on
[-1,1]
, we can check that

(5.17)

|(\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon-n^{-1/4},1-\epsilon+n^{-1/4}]}(G_{n}))^{2}-(\mathbb{E}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(G_{n}))^{2}|=O(n^{1/2-\delta^{\prime}}),

whenever
\delta^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4}
.
Then from the fact that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}N_{I_{1}}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{A_{n}}^{(1,0)}(x)R_{-}(x/\sqrt{n})^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{2,0}_{A_{n}}(x,y)R_{-}(x/\sqrt{n})R_{-}(y/\sqrt{n})dxdy

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}N_{I_{2}}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{A_{n}}^{(1,0)}(x)R_{+}(x/\sqrt{n})^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{2,0}_{A_{n}}(x,y)R_{+}(x/\sqrt{n})R_{+}(y/\sqrt{n})dxdy

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}N_{I_{3}}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{2},

we can apply (
5.13
) to do the swapping
O(n)
times with an overall cost
O(n^{1-\delta^{\prime}})
and obtain

(5.18)

\mathbb{E}N_{I_{2}}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{2}+O(n^{1-\delta^{\prime}}).

Combining the three estimates (
5.16
), (
5.17
), (
5.18
) above, we deduce that

\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{2}}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})\leq\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})+O(n^{1-\delta^{\prime}}).

We will prove, in Lemma (
5.3
), that

(5.19)

\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})=O(n^{\frac{1}{2}}).

This would imply
\operatorname{Var}N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})=O(n^{1-\delta^{\prime}})
.

Using Markov’s inequality with the expectation lower bound from (
5.15
), we conclude that we can find some
c^{\prime}>0
such that, with probability at least
1-n^{-c^{\prime}}
, we have
N_{\mathbb{R}}(A_{n})\geq N_{[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]}(A_{n}/\sqrt{n})\geq\sqrt{\frac{2n}{\pi}}(1-10\epsilon)-O(n^{1/2-c^{\prime}})
. This completes the proof upon changing the value of
\epsilon>0
.

∎

Finally, we prove the left-over computation (
5.19
)

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, the estimate (
5.19
) holds.

While the quantity
\operatorname{Var}N_{\mathbb{R}}(G_{n})
has been computed in
[
18
]
, this quantity does not immediately provide an upper bound for the variance of real roots in a certain interval. Therefore we have to follow the path in
[
18
]
and do the calculation again on this interval.

Proof.

We will use computations from
[
18
]
, see also
[
4
]
and
[
17
]
for a more detailed presentation. The variance
V_{n}
of
N_{\mathbb{R}}(G_{n})
can be computed via the integral of two-point correlation functions (see
[
18
]
, the lines between eq (17) and (18), or
[
3
]
, equation (3.2)). The precise formula is
V_{n}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}dx\int_{\mathbb{R}}dy\rho_{(2)}^{rT}(x,y)+E_{n}
where
E_{n}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{R}}(G_{n})
and
\rho_{(2)}^{rT}(x,y):=\rho_{(2)}^{r}(x,y)-\rho_{(1)}^{r}(x)\rho_{(1)}^{r}(y)
. It is not hard to check that we can restrict the integral of
x
and
y
to compact regions to get an estimate for
\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})
, in the form

\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})=\int_{\sqrt{n}I_{3}}dx\int_{\sqrt{n}I_{3}}dy\rho_{(2)}^{rT}(x,y)+\mathbb{E}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})

where
\sqrt{n}I_{3}
denotes the interval
\{\sqrt{n}x:x\in I_{3}\}
.
The two-point correlation kernel
\rho_{(2)}^{r}(x,y)
has the expression

\rho_{(2)}^{r}(x_{1},x_{2})=\operatorname{Pf}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{sgn}(x_{1}-x_{2})+I^{r}(x_{1},x_{2})&S^{r}(x_{1},x_{2})\\
-S^{r}(x_{1},x_{2})&D^{r}(x_{1},x_{2})\end{bmatrix},

where the exact expression of functions involved can be found in
[
18
]
, equation (15).
The large
n
limit of
\rho_{(2)}^{r}(x,y)
converges to, for fixed
x,y
, (see
[
4
]
, equation (2.36))

(5.20)

\operatorname{Pf}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}(y-x)e^{-(x-y)^{2}/2}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-(x-y)^{2}/2}\\
-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-(x-y)^{2}/2}&\operatorname{sgn}(x-y)\operatorname{erfc}(|x-y|/\sqrt{2})\end{bmatrix}

which has Gaussian tails and thus integrable over
\mathbb{R}
. From this expression we can readily compute that
\operatorname{Var}N_{I_{3}}(G_{n})=O(n^{\frac{1}{2}})
.

∎
